 <script>
/* 60fps bandwidth fix A (docs/32): switch off Framer link prefetch.
   Framer prefetches every linked page and runs that page's CMS queries in the background, which
   downloads the whole Shots collection (about 0.9 MB) on almost every visit. Framer skips prefetch
   when the browser reports Save-Data, and reads that flag nowhere else, so this reports it. */
(function () {
  try {
    var real = navigator.connection || {};
    var fake = { saveData: true, effectiveType: real.effectiveType || '4g', downlink: real.downlink, rtt: real.rtt,
                 addEventListener: function () {}, removeEventListener: function () {} };
    Object.defineProperty(navigator, 'connection', { value: fake, configurable: true });
  } catch (e) {}
})();
</script>

 <script>
/* 60fps bandwidth fix B (docs/32): internal links do a normal page load.
   Framer otherwise builds the next page in the browser and downloads CMS data to do it (the whole
   Shots collection for many pages). A normal load arrives pre-rendered and needs no CMS download.
   Leaves alone: new-tab and modifier clicks, downloads, other sites, and same-page # links.
   Capture phase so it runs before Framer, no stopPropagation so analytics click tags still fire. */
(function () {
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    if (e.defaultPrevented || e.button !== 0 || e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey || e.shiftKey || e.altKey) return;
    var t = e.target, a = t && t.closest ? t.closest('a[href]') : null;
    if (!a || (a.target && a.target !== '_self') || a.hasAttribute('download')) return;
    var u; try { u = new URL(a.getAttribute('href'), location.href); } catch (x) { return; }
    if (u.origin !== location.origin) return;
    if (u.pathname === location.pathname && u.search === location.search) return;
    e.preventDefault();
    location.assign(u.href);
  }, true);
})();
</script>
